T72 names no tag now; when the judgement returns, an amendment to this entry names its tag and its base before the first candidate.

## The target of a judgement in code

**R66. The target of a judgement in code: the judgement file names it by `"target"`, and `sim judge` takes it only where the table `targets` of `internal/criterion` records it for the judgement — `model-fair-1` with `M11.same` —, refusing `model-fair-1` without it, any other judgement with one, and a candidate that names a group or a goal beside it. The target is read off the pairs `fair` reads, with the numbers of `M11` as they are; it alone decides "better" and the choice, and the report writes it with `M11` of both grades, the model's score as a reference, and the bounds of R55 for the confirmation. The row `model-fair-1` — `held-out-model-fair-1` is in `internal/seed/sets.go`.** Decided by the executor 2026-11-16 in the planning of T71, in a run «Сделай эпик E…» of `spec`'s orchestrator, where nobody approves a plan (R156 spec): how the judgement file names the target is T71's planning (R65), and no document reserves it for the author.
- **What is built:**
  - the field `"target"` of the judgement file and `criterion.Input.Target`; the table `targets`, judgement → target, beside the code that reads it, as the table of sets is beside the seeds (R52). A target the table does not record for the judgement, or none where it records one, is refused with both named; so is a candidate of a judgement with a target that names `"group"` or `"goal"`: the judgement's target is every candidate's, and the constraints of R65 are the model's without a goal;
  - `criterion.Target`, read in `judge` beside `fair` for every candidate and for the confirmation: for each grade of `population.Fairness` but the reference's, `measure.ReadPair` of `M11.answers`, `M11.same` and `M11.band` as they are — not turned by their direction — on the pairs of `bench.Fair`, resampled by the reference's cell; Δg is the difference of `M11.same` turned, the candidate's less the base's, written 0 − x so that none is −0; the target's score is the mean of the grades' Δ, the k-th resample of each added up, and its interval the 51st lowest and highest of the 2,000. A grade whose cell the runs do not have leaves the score unread, and an unread score is not better; a grade on more than one graph is refused, since the mean is over grades, not cells, and the populations of fairness are on olympiad maths alone (R55). The bounds of R55 are read by the one function `bounds` that `sim evaluate` reads them by;
  - the verdict: `Better` is the lower end of the target's interval above zero, and nothing else; A*, its equals and the paired difference of two candidates are by the target's score and its resamples, `Verdict.deciding`; the checks — `I1`–`I4`, the floors, "no worse", `neutral` and `fair` — are those of the model's criterion as they were, and the model's score is read as before;
  - the report: a paragraph under its heading names the target and says that it alone decides; each candidate's lines give the target's score, then the model's score as "a reference that decides nothing" and each group's score, as R65 asks, and a table of `M11` on both grades — the base's, the candidate's, and the candidate's less the base's, each with its interval, the row `M11.same` being Δg; the confirmation adds a table of the bounds of R55 on the candidate's numbers, grade by grade, and whether the chosen one holds them there. Those numbers are the ones `sim evaluate` would read off the candidate's run, interval and all;
  - the row `model-fair-1` — `held-out-model-fair-1`, seed 20261116, of `internal/seed/sets.go` (the 2026-11-16 amendment to R04);
  - the tests, on synthetic files of results: the target chooses a candidate fairer on both grades over one that mends grade 5 alone, a candidate better by the score alone is not better, one that mends grade 5 at grade 1's cost fails `fair`; the numbers are the evaluation's; the confirmation by the target on `held-out-model-fair-1`, with and without the bounds; the target unread without the cells; the refusals; `sim judge` of a file with `"target"`.
  - No bit of a run moves: `just gate exact` gives the 69 numbers, and the base's file of 68 cells and `paths.json` stay. The files of `judgements/` name no target and read as before.

— *Why:*
- **The file names it, the table holds it.** Whoever reads `judgement.json` sees what the judgement goes by, and the table holds it to what the criterion wrote down before the first candidate, as the table of sets holds the confirmation (R52): a copy of the file kept for `model-3` with the target is refused, and so is a file of `model-fair-1` that left it out and would be judged by the score.
- **As they are.** Δ is the share of learners the candidate gains, more being better; through the shortfall it would be (1 − a) − (1 − b), off by a few ulps from b − a. The candidate's numbers and bounds are then those of the evaluation.
- **No group or goal.** R65: "A candidate names no score group", and its constraints name no goal; a candidate that named one would be judged by two targets.

— *Rejected:*
- (b) the target derived from the judgement's name alone — the file would not say what it goes by;
- (c) the file alone, any judgement free to name a target — a round of the model's criterion could go by a target its criterion never wrote down;
- `M11.same` as a score group of its own — a group weighs shares of paths, and `M11` has no path (R65);
- Δ read off the directed numbers of `fair` — the ulps above, and the report would need the shares as they are anyway.
- one reading of the pairs for `fair` and the target, `fair` turning the shares itself — the differences of `fair` would move by a few ulps, and with them, at the edge, a check of a judgement already recorded; the target reads the two cells of pairs a second time instead, 2,000 resamples each, a small part of a judgement's time.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-16 amendments to R25, R52 and R65. The merge releases the tag that `model-fair-1` pins, which T72 names by an amendment to R65 (R29; the 2026-11-16 amendment to R57). Where it is written: RUN.md, T71 ([docs/plan/E10.md](plan/E10.md)); `internal/criterion/`, `internal/seed/sets.go`, `main.go`, the tests; README, "Judging a candidate"; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3, 3.6 and 3.8.

— *Amendment 2026-11-16 (T77; R69):* the judgement `model-fair-1` is deferred, so T72 names no tag now: the tag of T71 is the first that can judge it, and when the judgement returns, an amendment to R65 names its tag, that of T71 or a later one. The code of the target stays.

## The gate as jobs

**R67. CI runs the gate as jobs: the workflow `.github/workflows/gate.yml`, which `ci.yml` calls once an architecture and a caller can call by a tag of `sim`, runs four jobs of `just gate-part <k> 4` and after them one of `just gate-check`, named as the gate was, "Gate / amd64" and "Gate / arm64". The gate's Go cache is keyed by `go.sum` and the Dockerfile, with `restore-keys`, and only a push saves it. `TestEngineOnRuns` is subtests, its learners in processes of the test binary where it can start them and coverage is not counted. What the gate and the tests prove does not change.** Decided by the executor 2026-11-16 in T74, in a run «Сделай эпик E…» of `spec`'s orchestrator (R156 spec), by row 7.19 of COORDINATION.md spec and the sixth 2026-11-15 amendment to R76 spec; the author's word of 2026-11-16 — the speed of CI now, in the run of T171 spec. How it is built is the executor's: no document reserves it for the author.
- **The parts.** Job k of n runs, as processes, the parts k + n·j of n·p, j below p, with p its processors, so the parts of all the jobs are each part of n·p once; `gate-check` builds the workspace again, holds the build to `gate.json` and merges the parts by `bench.Merge`, which refuses a part missing, repeated or of another run — another set, learners, libraries or number of parts —, and hands `sim gate check` the commits of its build, so that parts run on other libraries are refused. A learner comes from its seeds alone (architecture 3.4), so the merged file is that of `just gate`, and so are the numbers: on 2026-11-16 `just gate-check exact` over four `just gate-part` in the container gave the table of `just gate exact` to the byte. `just gate` itself is the same run, as one job.
- **The check.** It runs when a part failed too, `!cancelled()`, and then fails: a required check skipped would pass. Its name is the gate's, so the ruleset of `main` requires what it did and no setting changes; a part's is "Gate / amd64 / part k".
- **Four jobs.** The gate took 424 s of "Gate / amd64", the slowest job with "Go / targets", 317 s (measurements 3.1 spec), and its run is most of it. On this pull request four parts took, the slowest part and the check, 215 s on amd64 and 175 s on arm64, against 502 s and 362 s on `main`, and two 404 s and 254 s, with a part of 319 s on a slow x64 runner (measurements 1.1). The jobs are more — ten for the gate, not two — in the queue of the organization, whose runners all its repositories share: in one run DCO waited about 5 minutes to start. T75 weighs the number again once row 7.13 spec shortens "Go / targets".
- **The caches.** The image of `toolchain` comes by the steps of R63, and on a miss only the first part saves it, so that the parts do not save one key; the check saves nothing. The Go cache was keyed by `gate.json` too, with no `restore-keys`: 54 entries of 55 MiB, 2,289 MiB (measurements 3.1 spec). Now a new pin builds on the entry before it, a pull request only reads, and the first part of a push saves when its key had no entry. The entry is not refreshed until `go.sum` or the Dockerfile changes: what a new pin adds is built again by each job.
- **For callers.** `gate.yml` takes `sim-tag` and `sim-commit` — the tag must be on the commit —, `library`, the variable whose library the caller's checkout replaces, `GATE_LEARNER_MODEL` or `GATE_ENGINE`, `mode` and `runner`. The three come together or not at all, and the check holds `sim-commit` to the tag and to the SHA the caller's `uses:` names in its workflow, `github.workflow_ref`: Dependabot moves the SHA, not the inputs. It is a path of the release and in its archive (R29), so a caller takes it by a tag. Taking it is rows 7.20 and 7.21 spec, in the callers.
- **`TestEngineOnRuns`.** It took 16.8 s of the 20.4 s of `internal/measure`, the longest package of `go test ./...`, 24.6 s in the container, nearly all of it F-model, 24 learners on olympiad maths; in js/wasm the package took 103 s of the 134 s of the target (measurements 3.1 spec). Each population is a subtest; where the binary can start processes, its learners go to as many processes as the processors, k mod n, each writing its learners' sums, and the test reads them, as the recipes split a run (R09). The output of a process goes into a file, so that `os/exec` starts no goroutine, and the tests start none (the 2026-11-09 amendment to R10); `os/exec` joins the tests' imports of `.github/coderules.json`. In js/wasm and wasip1, which start no process, and under coverage, which a process would not add to the profile, the learners run in the test, as before: "Go / test" and those two targets keep their time, the native targets gain. On 2026-11-16 the sums by processes were those of the test, learner by learner, for E-base and F-model.

— *Rejected:*
- `t.Parallel` subtests — goroutines in the tests bring back the race detector (the 2026-11-09 amendment to R10);
- fewer learners in F-model — it needs 24 to read every resample, and the test would prove less;
- the coverage of the processes merged into the profile — `go test` writes the profile of the one process, and a step of `ci-test` to merge profiles would be one the shared `go.yml` does not know of;
- a fixed number of processes a job — the runners of a gate are of one kind, and a job with other processors fails the check loudly, by the number of parts;
- two jobs — on this pull request a part took 319 s on one x64 runner and 206 s on another, so the gate came within a minute of "Go / arm64", and a slow runner would make it the slowest job again; and once row 7.13 spec shortens "Go / targets", four would be needed anyway;
- the Go cache keyed by `gate.json` too, the key of R67 its `restore-keys` — row 7.19 spec keeps `gate.json` out of the key.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-16 amendments to R26, R29, R63 and R64. What waits for other rows — the move to the release of `.github` of row 7.13 spec, and the tag of `spec` of row 7.14 spec with the stage `toolchain-core`, the last, since it changes `.devcontainer/` — is T75 and T76. Where it is written: RUN.md, T74 ([docs/plan/E10.md](plan/E10.md)); `.github/workflows/gate.yml`, `ci.yml`, `release.yml`, `.github/coderules.json`; the `justfile`; `internal/measure/parts_test.go`, `engine_test.go`; README, "Gate"; [architecture.md](architecture.md), section 3.9; [measurements.md](measurements.md), section 1.1.

The number of parts, weighed again on the times of `.github` v0.1.30, stays four (R68, «The parts»).

The gate stays in four parts and in the stage `toolchain`.

The step checks out `sim` itself: in `sim`'s own CI the repository at the event's ref, as `go.yml` does; in a caller's, `altnovatrail/sim` at `sim-commit`, a full SHA that the step "Inputs" holds to `^[0-9a-f]{40}$`. A pull request's code runs there on purpose — the gate is the check of a candidate's code — in a workflow without privileges: `permissions: contents: read`, `persist-credentials: false`, no secret, and only a push to `main` saves a cache. No change closes the finding but dropping the checkout the gate exists for, and the mark "false positive" or "won't fix" would hide a new finding of the same rule (R33 spec). ## CI on `.github` v0.1.30

**R68. CI of `sim` calls the shared checks of `.github` at v0.1.30, the release of row 7.13 COORDINATION.md spec without its last task, by the SHA of its tag, 8311cbf, in every `uses:` of `.github/workflows/`: the caches of Go in `go.yml`, one file of the image of `toolchain`, the targets as jobs of a matrix and `fma-diff` at once, the signatures and the check of the release at once, and CodeQL's Go from the archive the Dockerfile pins. "Gate" takes the image by the steps of that `go.yml` — the file `novatrail-toolchain.tar`, the image `novatrail-toolchain`, the key the same —, and a push to `main` alone saves it, by the first part, and the gate's Go cache too. The recipe `test-targets` runs the script of the job `target` on the targets `go.yml` names for the host. The gate stays in four parts. What the checks prove does not change.** Decided by the executor 2026-11-16 in T75, in a run «Сделай эпик E…» of `spec`'s orchestrator (R156 spec), by rows 7.13 and 7.19 of COORDINATION.md spec and the word of `spec`'s coordinator (T171 spec) to take v0.1.30 now; no document reserves it for the author.
- **What v0.1.30 brings** (sources 5.4): T60–T65 of epic E21 `.github`, R41–R45 `.github`. No input of the workflows `sim` calls changes, and the checks of the ruleset keep their names: "Go / targets" is now the check after the jobs "Go / target (…)", one a target, and "Go / toolchain" and "Go / target (…)" are no checks of it. The jobs of `go.yml` take the caches of Go and the image from this repository's cache, and a push to `main` alone saves either; `release.yml` signs and checks eight files at once; CodeQL takes Go from go.dev by `ARG GO_VERSION` and `ARG GO_SHA256_AMD64` of the stage `toolchain`, which `sim`'s Dockerfile has, with `ENV` of `PATH` and `GOTOOLCHAIN` alone.
- **What is changed here:** the seven references, `fab102d # v0.1.24` → `8311cbf # v0.1.30`; in `gate.yml` the steps of the image in the parts and the check — the file, the image, the warning and the condition of the save of `go.yml` v0.1.30, as `arm64` has them, with `sim/` before the Dockerfile's path; `docker run` of `novatrail-toolchain`; the recipe `test-targets`, since the job `targets` of v0.1.30 runs no script: it takes the script of the job `target`, and the targets from `go.yml` — on amd64 the matrix of `target`, on another host the `TARGETS` of `arm64` —, run in order in one container, as `arm64` runs them; the comments of `ci.yml` and `codeql.yml`; [.claude/rules/ci.md](../.claude/rules/ci.md).
- **The caches of the gate.** One entry still serves "Gate" and `go.yml`, now of the file of R42 `.github`, which `documents.yml` reads and saves too. On a push to `main` after a miss, the first part, `go.yml` — `arm64` on arm64, the job `toolchain` on x64 — and on x64 `documents.yml` save one key, and the later get "Unable to reserve cache", a warning; all build the same image by the same command (R42 `.github`, «One entry»). A pull request saves nothing: its entry would be read by its own runs alone and crowd out `main`'s (R42 `.github`). So on a miss the four parts of each architecture build the image each, at once, and the check after them builds it again, where before it loaded the file the first part had saved in the pull request's scope: a build of the stage, 20–25 s (measurements 1.1), within the check's 15 minutes, and only on a pull request that changes the Dockerfile — T76. The gate's Go cache is saved by a push to `main` alone too, where any event but a pull request saved it: in `sim` only a push to `main` reaches the gate, and at a caller an entry of another ref would be read by that ref alone.
- **For callers.** The gate keys the image by `sim`'s Dockerfile, so at a caller no job of its own `go.yml` reads or saves that entry: its gate saves the image and the Go cache on a push to its `main` alone, and its pull requests read them, where before each pull request saved its own image. A caller that runs the gate on no push to `main` builds the image in each job of the gate, as on a miss. Taking `gate.yml` is rows 7.20 and 7.21 spec, in the callers (R67).
- **The parts.** On pull request #81 (measurements 1.1) the longest job is "Go / arm64", 452 s and the end of the run, as on `main`; the targets, a job each, took 71–245 s, and "Go / targets" ended at 6 min 42 s, its jobs started late by the busy runners. The gate in four parts ended at 4 min 13 s on amd64 and 3 min 52 s on arm64. Two parts took 404 s on a slow x64 runner in T74, 6.7 minutes, within a minute of "Go / arm64", and three would save runner time only by the overhead of a job, about 40 s, for about a minute more of the gate. The gate is also the callers' job, by a tag (R67), where nothing else is as long: so four stay.
- **`toolchain-core`.** The question the task left to planning does not arise: v0.1.30 runs no check in `toolchain-core`. T66 `.github` will, and «у Dockerfile без этой стадии — как прежде» (sources 5.4), so it holds even before `sim` has the stage; T66 waits for `.github`'s move to the tag of `spec` of row 7.14, and that move changes `.devcontainer/`, which by the word of the coordinator (T171 spec) does not change at night. T76 takes the release with T66 together with the tag of `spec`: the SHA, the steps of the image if T66 gives them a new file, and the recipes.

- a job `toolchain` of the gate's own, as `go.yml` has: on a hit it adds a job before the parts on every run (R42 `.github`), for the builds of a miss alone, which only a change of the Dockerfile brings — T76;

- the check taking the image of the first part by an artifact of the run, as the jobs of `go.yml` take that of its job `toolchain`: an upload of about 489 MB (R42 `.github`) on every miss and a step more in the part, for a build of 20–25 s;
- two or three parts: «The parts» above.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-16 amendments to R10, R29, R63 and R67. The merge changes `gate.yml` and the justfile, paths of the release, and so releases a tag of `sim` with the same numbers (R29). Its first push to `main` saves the entries of the Go caches of `go.yml` and the image of arm64 of the new file; until then a pull request misses them. ## The pin of `spec`

**R71. The Go module `spec` that `go.mod`, `go.sum` and `gate.json` name is the tag of the submodule `spec/`, and a move to a tag changes them together, with the lines of CLAUDE.md and of the opening of RUN.md that name the tag; README.md names none (R21 spec). The move is its commit, which says why, and no entry of the log (R160 spec). A tag that changes no code the simulator builds keeps the bits: `just gate exact` holds all 69 numbers, the bands are not written anew, and the paths and the runs of the base stay. `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json` sets `"shutdownAction": "none"`, and the Dockerfile's comments on the stage `toolchain` and on gopls are the template's. Tasks outside an epic stand in table 1 of the current epic, not in `run/TASKS.md` (R61).** Decided by the executor in T78, a run «Сделай эпик E…» of `spec`'s orchestrator (R156 spec); where the tag is named and that a move has no entry, by R160 spec in T84.

— *Why:*
- the Go module `spec` is pinned at the tag of the submodule (R64 spec): what the simulator reads of `spec.go`, `format/`, `types/` and `contracts/` and what `docs-check-repo` checks the documents against are one version;
- a tag that changes no code the simulator builds moves no number, so a move runs no decisive run, and `just gate exact` shows it;
- `"shutdownAction": "none"`: closing the window of VS Code no longer stops the container under a running executor; `docker stop` ends it (R156 spec);
- the version lives in the submodule and `go.mod` (R160 spec): an entry per move, with amendments, restated it in up to seven entries at once.

— *Rejected:*
- `go.mod` and `gate.json` left on an older tag while nothing the simulator reads changed — the module and the submodule would differ;
- an entry of the log for each move, and amendments to the entries that name the tag — what R160 spec ends;
- the tag named in README.md or in a status paragraph of [architecture.md](architecture.md) — a README names no version (R21 spec), and a status paragraph is a chronicle (R160 spec);
- `run/TASKS.md` — R61;
- the Dockerfile's comments kept as they were, to spare the cached image the miss its new hash gives — the copy would drift from the template.

— *Consequence:* `spec/`, `go.mod`, `go.sum`, `gate.json`; CLAUDE.md, "Documents"; the opening of RUN.md; `.devcontainer/`. A merge that changes `go.mod` or `gate.json`, paths of the release, releases a tag of `sim` with the same numbers (R29). A rebuild of the container is the author's step, «Rebuild Container», when `.devcontainer/` changes.

## The criterion of the short run of the three rules

**R72. The criterion of the short run of the three rules of R157 spec, written down before the run. The rules — `learner-model` at the head of its pull request #84, df338e5 — are checked when all four hold, on a run of 2,000 working learners a cell of `D-grade1`, `D-grade3`, `D-grade5` and a new cell `C-grade1`, against the cells `D-` of the base's run of T64: grade 5's `M11.same` above 0.5525 at the low end of its interval; `fair` of R57 on grades 1 and 5; the guard's false firings, `M12.false` of `D-grade3`, at most 0.05 at the high end of its interval; and the adult's correction, `C-grade1`, whose `M11.same` is above that of `D-grade1` in the same run. The report is `evaluations/rules-1/`, and its numbers go «в реестр spec» for dpia 10.2 spec.** Decided by the executor 2026-11-17 in T79, by the text of row 4.21 of COORDINATION.md spec that `spec`'s orchestrator gave (R156 spec; rule 6 of COORDINATION.md spec): the three bounds are the author's, in the amendment of T173 to R157 spec, and the cell of the correction and the reading of the bounds are `sim`'s to choose and write in the criterion, as the row says.
- **The candidate and the base.** The candidate is `learner-model` at df338e58e35f97c51eca49be0064f19d0fcfc0a2, the head of the branch `t68-three-rules` of pull request #84 — ef7c34f, the code of the rules (R45 learner-model), and a commit of its plan alone —, with `engine` v0.1.6 and the packages of `gate.json`: the commit, not a release, by the author's answer of 2026-11-16 to row В54 of `learner-model`. The base is `learner-model` v0.1.8 with `engine` v0.1.6, its lines of the cells `D-` in `runs/base-t64.jsonl`, the file `paths.json` names, SHA-256 `9e89cb7c…7763` (R58).
- **The run.** Short, by R157 spec: the four populations alone on olympiad maths, 2,000 working learners a cell — the size of a decisive run, so the numbers of grades 1 and 5 have the intervals of R58 —, under the engine, with the invariants. Not the 68 cells: none of the other 65 cells has a bound here, and the gate's bands read them. The learner n is the same learner as the base's learner n in all four cells (R55).
- **1. Better.** `M11.same` of `D-grade5/novatrail/olympiad-math` against `D-grade3`, by the 50th answer as R55 reads it, at the low end of its 95 % interval above 0.5525, the base's value in R58. Under the rules the model starts a band below the band of the grade: grade 5 on the truth's band, grade 3 a band below it, and grade 1 at −2.5, below the lowest band.
- **2. Nowhere worse.** `fair` of R57 as it is: on `D-grade1` and `D-grade5`, the shortfall of `M11.same`, `M11.answers` and `M11.band` each no worse than the base's, the worse end of the 95 % interval of the paired difference within the larger of 0.01, one answer, 0.01 and 4.3 standard errors — six checks, each held.
- **3. The guard's false firings.** `M12.false` is read on `D-grade3`, whose truth is the band of its grade, so a guard that lowers its level lowers a level already a band below the truth: the share of the learners on whose run the guard fired at least once, with its 95 % interval from 2,000 resamples of the learners' n. A firing is a piece of evidence after which the count of the guard of the module, `FailuresInARow` of the model's state, stands at 3 and stood at 2 before it: where R45 learner-model lowers the level by a band. A learner at the floor −2.5, whom the guard does not lower, counts too, so the share is an upper bound. It holds at most 0.05 at the high end of its interval; it is read on the whole run, not on its first 200 answers alone.
- **4. The adult's correction.** `C-grade1` is `D-grade1` — its assumptions, grade 1 and the truth of grade 3, and the unit of `D-grade3` — with an adult who sets the module level, once, to the band of the truth, place 2, the band of grades 3–4: by the evidence `LevelCorrection` of `learner-model`, right after the attempt that gives the learner's 10th first answer, at the time of its last action — about one session, as R55 counts fifty answers five sessions. It is the cell where the rules leave the start furthest from the truth, five logits below it, and the adult who sees a child ahead of their grade raises the level in one step. Its `M11` is read against `D-grade3` as the grades' are, and it holds when the 95 % interval of the paired difference of `M11.same`, `C-grade1`'s less `D-grade1`'s of the same run, has its low end above zero: the correction brings the estimate to that of grade 3 by the 50th answer for more learners than no correction does. `M11.answers` and `M11.band` of the cell are reported without a bound.
- **The outcome.** All four hold: the rules are checked by `sim`, for dpia 8 spec and row 4.21, and `gate.json` and `go.mod` move to the candidate's commit with the gate's bands recorded on it, so that its merge releases the tag `learner-model` raises its "Gate" to (R103 spec). One fails: the result is recorded as it is, the bands are not recorded, and what follows is the author's, a row of table 3. The bounds are not changed after the run (R163 standalone).
- **The report.** `sim evaluate` reads `evaluations/rules-1/evaluation.json` — the run of the rules and the base's run, each by its SHA-256 — and writes `report.md`: the criterion citing this decision, the libraries of both sides, the numbers with their intervals, each of the four with whether it holds, and a paragraph for dpia 10.2 spec. It is built at a tag, as R58 asks of an evaluation, or names the version of its build.

— *Why:*
- **The low end, not the value.** "Above 0.5525" from the base's own run says the rules are better only when the interval says so; R55 reads its bounds at the end of the interval as well.
- **`D-grade3` for the false firings.** The row asks for the cell of the third grade whose truth is the band of its grade: `D-grade3` is the one cell whose truth and grade are of one band by construction, the reference of `M11`, and its learners already run here. A firing there is false by definition: the level stands below the truth from the start.
- **Fired at least once.** A learner whom the guard lowers once has met a level two bands below the truth; counting firings rather than learners would weigh a learner twice.
- **Grade 1 for the correction.** Under the rules the start of grade 5 is the truth's band, so an adult has nothing to correct there; grade 1 starts five logits below it, the slowest the rules leave. Ten answers are the first time an adult has seen the child practise, past the trial series of five; a correction to the truth's band is what an adult who knows the child sets.
- **Against `D-grade1` of the same run.** The base has no correction, so the paired difference with the same learners without it says what the correction alone does.

— *Rejected:*
- the 68 cells of a decisive run — 45 minutes for numbers no bound reads, against "short" of R157 spec;
- a run on the release of row 4.20 — the author's answer to В54 of `learner-model`: the release waits for the bands of `sim`;
- `M11.same` above 0.5525 by its value alone — see "Why";
- the false firings on the populations `G` of grade-3 maths — their truth is drawn around the band of the grade, not on it, and a firing there may be true;
- the correction as "Harder" one band at a time — the evidence sets the band the adult chose, and two clicks give the same band;
- a correction in `D-grade5` — the rules start it on the truth's band already.

— *Consequence:* T79 builds `C-grade1`, the count of the guard in the trace of a learner of fairness, `M12.false` and the evaluation of the rules in `sim evaluate`, runs the cells and writes `evaluations/rules-1/`; the numbers and the outcome are an amendment to this entry. Д11 of [run/DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md) is this task. Where it is written: RUN.md, T79; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3, 3.2, 3.5 and 3.6.

— *Amendment 2026-11-17 (T79), the result, recorded as it is:* **the rules are not checked by this criterion: conditions 1 and 3 hold, 2 and 4 do not.** The run: `sim run -populations D-grade1,D-grade3,D-grade5,C-grade1 -learners 2000` in 20 parts and `sim merge`, built at 4f64534 of this branch with `learner-model` v0.1.13-0.20261116235821-df338e58e35f, 8,000 lines, SHA-256 `8b1d9dcd…2c16`, 3 min 55 s by the clock on 20 processors, the load average 13.63 at its start. The report is `evaluations/rules-1/` (R56), built at 502c93a, which changed only the report's writing:
  1. grade 5's `M11.same` is 0.812 [0.795; 0.829] against the base's 0.5525 — holds;
  2. `fair` holds five of its six checks and fails one: `M11.band` of `D-grade1` is 0.0177 [0.0146; 0.021] against the base's 0.0067, worse by at most 0.0142 against the tolerance 0.01. The bound of R55, 0.05, still holds;
  3. `M12.false` is 0.0005 [0; 0.0015], one learner of 2,000 — holds;
  4. `C-grade1`'s `M11.same` is 0.412 [0.3905; 0.4335] against `D-grade1`'s 0.909 [0.8965; 0.9215], the difference −0.497 [−0.519; −0.475] — does not hold. The adult corrected all 2,000 learners.

  What the run shows, read off the traces (a one-off reading of the run's file, not a criterion): under the rules each grade's series of levels is the base's series of the grade a band below it, to the last digit — grade 5's under the rules is grade 3's under the base, grade 3's is grade 1's — so `M11` of grade 5 under the rules is that of grade 1 under the base. The estimate of grade 3, whose truth is its band, climbs from 0.44 at the first answer to 2.04 by the 50th and 2.15 by the 200th, against the truth's mean 2.5; the corrected learners of `C-grade1` stand at 2.50 from the 11th answer on and 2.41 by the 200th. Condition 4 compares them with grade 3, which lags the truth, so the measure counts a correction that brings the level to the truth as a departure from the reference: the measure this entry chose reads agreement with grade 3, not with the truth. Condition 2 fails because grade 1 now starts at −2.5, below the lowest band, and its tasks after the 50th answer differ from grade 3's more than under the base.

  By "The outcome" above, `gate.json` stays on `learner-model` v0.1.8 and no bands are recorded; `go.mod` names the candidate's pseudo-version, since the count of the guard reads its state. What follows is the author's: rows В31 and В32 of table 3. The bounds and the measures of this entry are not changed (R163 standalone). The code is described in sections 3, 3.2 and 3.5 of [architecture.md](architecture.md), not in 3.6, whose budget it would pass, and in README.

В32 — the correction is checked anew by a criterion of its own against the truth, written down before its run, R73, on a short run of two cells, `evaluations/rules-2/`; `rules-1` stays recorded as it is, and the numbers of both go «в реестр spec» for dpia 10.2 spec.** Both by the recommendations, through `spec`'s orchestrator (R156 spec). "The outcome" above, which held the bands back when a condition failed, gives way to the answer to В31 for this run alone; the bands are a decision of their own.

## The criterion of the adult's correction against the truth

**R73. The adult's correction of R72 is checked anew against the truth, by a criterion written down before its run: on a short run of `C-grade1` and `D-grade1` alone, 2,000 working learners a cell, `learner-model` at the same commit df338e5, the share of the learners whose module level θ holds within 0.5 of the truth's `θ*` for ten answers in a row by the 50th answer, `M13.same`, is higher under the correction: the 95 % interval of the paired difference, `C-grade1`'s less `D-grade1`'s, has its low end above zero. The report is `evaluations/rules-2/`; `rules-1` stays as it is.** The author's answer 2026-11-17 to row В32, by the recommendation, through `spec`'s orchestrator (R156 spec); the reading of it in code is the executor's.
- **The measure.** `M13` reads one learner against their own truth, as `M3` catches up: k is the answers before the first of ten in a row at which `|θ − θ*|` is below 0.5, θ the model's module level after each answer as `M11` takes it and `θ*` the truth's module level after it, `run.Obs.TrueLevel`; a learner for whom it never holds within the first 200 answers counts as 200. `M13.answers` is the mean of k, `M13.same` the share of learners with k at most 50. The trace of a learner of fairness carries `θ*` after each answer, `truth`, beside the levels.
- **The cells.** The learner n of `C-grade1` and of `D-grade1` is one learner, of one truth and one unit (R55), so the difference is paired; the resamples are drawn by the name of the reference's cell, as for `M11`. Only these two cells run: neither the base nor the other grades read `M13` here.
- **The outcome.** It holds or not, recorded as it is, and it does not change what the answer to В31 says: the bands are recorded by the answer to В31 either way. The bounds are not changed after the run (R163 standalone).

— *Why:* the correction is for bringing the estimate to where the child is; `M11` read it against grade 3, whose estimate lags the truth under the rules (the 2026-11-17 amendment to R72), and the truth is what the virtual learner knows and a real one does not. `D-grade1` without the correction is the same learners, so the difference is the correction's alone.

— *Rejected:* `rules-1` read anew with the new measure — its file has no `θ*`, and a criterion read off a run already looked at would be a criterion after the run; the difference of θ and `θ*` after the 50th answer alone — one cut is noisier and gives no number of answers (R55); all four cells again — `M13` has no bound on the grades here, and the run would cost more than it gives.

— *Consequence:* T79 builds `M13` and the trace's `truth`, runs the two cells and writes `evaluations/rules-2/`; its numbers are an amendment to this entry. Where it is written: RUN.md, T79; [architecture.md](architecture.md), sections 3.2 and 3.5; README.

— *Amendment 2026-11-17 (T79), the result, recorded as it is:* **the correction holds this criterion: `M13.same` of `C-grade1` is 0.56 [0.54; 0.581] against `D-grade1`'s 0.351 [0.3295; 0.372], the difference 0.209 [0.185; 0.233]; `M13.answers` is 76.49 [73.16; 79.77] against 119.7 [116.1; 123.5], 43.24 answers fewer [38.87; 47.32].** The run: `sim run -populations D-grade1,C-grade1 -learners 2000` in 20 parts and `sim merge`, built at 2095e69 of this branch, 4,000 lines, SHA-256 `552eaff7…23c0`, 1 min 55 s by the clock, the load average 1.78 at its start. Past the field `truth` of the trace its lines are byte for byte those of the two cells in the run of `rules-1` (`cmp`). The adult corrected all 2,000 learners. The report is `evaluations/rules-2/`.

— *Amendment 2026-11-17 (T81; R75):* the correction of `C-grade1` is one fact of `LevelCorrection` to place 2, and `SetModuleLevel` of `spec` v0.18.14 takes one band at a time. For a learner whose level stands below place 2 when the correction comes, the contract's steps end in the state of `sim`'s one fact, so this criterion and its numbers read the correction as the contract gives it; for one whose level already stands in place 2 or above, they differ, and the share of such learners is not measured — Д13 of [run/DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md).

Taking it is a change to the checkout and the caches of the workflow every caller runs, larger than this move, and for nothing while the steps are the same: Д14 returns to it when a release of `.github` changes them, in `go.yml` or in the action. Nor does the gate take the drop of the modules of T75 `.github`: its entry restored by the prefix keeps them, so it grows by a pin's modules at each move — the same debt, since the action would bring the drop with it.
- **What is changed here (T83):** `spec/` — v0.18.27; `go.mod` and `go.sum` — the Go module `spec` v0.18.27; `gate.json` — `spec` v0.18.27, the bands not written anew; `.github/workflows/` — `ci.yml`, `release.yml`, `codeql.yml`, `scorecard.yml` on v0.1.33, f1c7e9e, and the comments of `gate.yml`; README.md and the new `docs/README.md`; CLAUDE.md; RUN.md — its opening and the paragraph «Language»; the justfile and `.claude/rules/gate.md`, their citations; [architecture.md](architecture.md), the status, sections 2 and 3 and "What stays open"; row В33 of table 3 deleted and the new В34, Д13 and the new Д14 of table 4; [sources.md](sources.md), records 5.6 and 7.13.
- **The bits are the same:** `just gate exact` on v0.18.27 — all 69 numbers to the bit; the tag changes no code the simulator builds, so no decisive run is run again.

— *Why:* the parallel work of wave 4 puts every repository on the latest tag of `spec` and the latest release of `.github` while `app` runs its epic (R156 spec), and the move holds in one review: the pins, the README by the template, the row the tag closes.

— *Rejected:*
- the README's sections dropped where the justfile or the rules say them — a caller reads the form of the call there, not the rules of `sim`, and moved whole they need no new reading;
- the gate on the action `toolchain` in this task — see above; Д14;
- `docs/README.md` split, the gate and the judgement in documents of their own — the template allows it, but one move keeps the citations by section the same.

— *Consequence:* the 2026-11-18 amendments to R10, R27, R29, R34, R49, R57, R60, R61, R63, R67, R68, R70, R71, R72, R75 and R76. The merge changes `go.mod` and `gate.json`, paths of the release, and so releases a tag of `sim` with the same numbers (R29). `.devcontainer/` does not change, so no rebuild of the container.
